File: Aerosol of my Love.mp3
Title: Aerosol of my Love
Feel: Bright, Grooving, Relaxed
Genre: Pop
Album: Medium Electronic (Electronic and Rock)
Length: 2:22
BPM: 100
Instruments: Synths, Drums
Description: The title of this piece of music is a pun. The word "Aerosol" is a reference to a common hair product delivery method of the 1980s. "Aerosol" can very easily sound like the words "Here is all" or even "There is all". One can effectively hear the title spoken out loud as "Here is all of my Love". But they would be wrong. I don't like the usage of aerosols in applications where there are other delivery methods available. #ClawBangs

File: Canon in D for Two Harps.mp3
Title: Canon in D for Two Harps
Feel: Bright, Calming, Relaxed
Genre: Classical
Album: Touching Moments (Lighter Faire)
Length: 5:40
BPM: 83
Instruments: Concert Harps
Description: Harpists, by nature, are solitary people who love horses. Getting harpists to play fast together and contour a melodic line is neigh-on impossible. So we have to give all the notes to one person so it doesn't sound like harp hell. But now you need to rest, because plucking like that is insane, so the other person gets the next tough part. You also need to make sure that each part makes sense so you can practice it and not feel like you're throwing your soul down a well. I believe this is the best arrangement of this piece for 2 harps; A thing which should never have been tried. The arrangement itself is in the Public Domain, and sheet music is available.

File: Gregorian Chant.mp3
Title: Gregorian Chant
Feel: Mystical, Somber
Genre: Unclassifiable
Album: Thoughtful (Lighter Faire)
Length: 3:14
Instruments: Synth, Choir
Description: Is it a real Gregorian Chant? Nope. For one - there are no real words in it. Honestly, I don't want a religious exaltation mucking up my fantasy world. So if you're building a historically accurate "Monk Simulator: Year of Our Lord 882!", just move on. The other reason this isn't real is I never learned how to read that notation. There are insufficient sticks for the rhythms in the notation... I know, there is a real convention that you can learn. I just grabbed a few pieces of the sheet music from the 1200s, and guessed at how to do it. Sounds reasonable enough to me! (says the guy who knows virtually nothing about the art)

At 3:40 the piece finds it?s stride with pulsating synthesizer effects and a rumbling bass line. Best used as accompaniment for a tense, difficult level of a game or as the final battle with a villain. File: On Hold for You.mp3
Title: On Hold for You
Feel: Bright, Grooving, Relaxed
Genre: Jazz
Album: Jazz (Everything Else)
Length: 3:38
BPM: 115
Instruments: Tenor Sax, Electric Piano, Bass, Drums
Description: The tenor sax performance in this piece was done by an AI musician. The notes and chords were generated algorithmically (not by an AI). https://sites.research.google/tonetransfer 0:33 there is an amazingly smooth and correct sounding run-up. 0:50 it goes below the actual tenor sax range. I love how it extrapolated down there! 0:58 there's some leak hiss going on at 0:58. I'll call that "artistic license". 1:08 it goes quite a bit above the standard range for a tenor sax, but those notes should be available in altissimo with some fancy fingerings. 2:20 you can almost taste the spit! 2:58 it plays the same note back to back, but changes the tone of the note. Very cool. This ToneTransfer AI Sax is amazing. Getting this level of detail in a recording with standard virtual instruments is almost impossible. AI for the win!

When you go out playing an instrument in a band for money, that is called "gigging". Gigging is good for a trombone player... but man, you gotta be good. You pretty much need a bass player and a drummer... not many people need a trombone player, so they typically don't get many gigs... Did I explain that right? Wait, let me start over...

Description: I wrote this one December 27th, 2009 mainly for fun. It is a sweeping quiet piece until the past bit where is opens up a bit. File: Thunderbird.mp3
Title: Thunderbird
Feel: Somber, Relaxed, Mystical, Calming
Genre: Unclassifiable
Album: Wonders of Other Worlds (Everything Else)
Length: 6:02
Instruments: Bass Flute, Synths, Percussion
Description: Wikipedia describes the Thunderbird as "a supernatural bird of power and strength". It will also ask you to disambiguate it from the automobile made by Ford from 1955 to 2005. I'm sure it was a nice car. I've never owned one. But there were a lot of them! The late 70's Thunderbirds are particularly horrifying... They had those headlight covers that flipped up... because that was a good idea at some point. But this piece! Oh! This piece! Atmosphere like you wouldn't believe! The flute is just so earthy! The organic drones are also beautiful. The whole thing is beautiful. Kind of like you went to an alien day spa in another dimension where days last for years, but you still never get all wrinkly when you're in the bubbly warm waters. mmmm... bubbly warm waters... with the slight possibility of a serial killer.

Genre: Electronica
Album: Medium Electronic (Electronic and Rock)
Length: 3:16
BPM: 120
Instruments: Synth, Percussion
Description: You know that sound that was big in 80s dance music with that sort of galloping doo-do-do-DAH-do-do-doo-do-do-DAH thing? This has a thing like that, but in really, really complex drum sound. You need to wait until 16 seconds in to hear what the doo-do-do-DAH-do-do-doo-do-do-DAH sounds like. I know it can be difficult reading KRN (Kevin's Rhythmic Notation). A-do-do-dooo-a-da-da-daaah is all I want to say to you.
